Many authors see the roots of modern antisemitism in both pagan antiquity and early Christianity. Jerome Chanes identifies six stages in the historical development of antisemitism:[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChanes2004-162]

1. Pre-Christian anti-Judaism in ancient Greece and Rome, which was primarily ethnic in nature

2. Christian antisemitism in antiquity and the Middle Ages, which was religious in nature and has extended into modern times

3. Traditional Muslim antisemitism, which was—at least, in its classical form—nuanced in that Jews were a protected class

4. Political, social and economic antisemitism of Enlightenment and post-Enlightenment Europe, which laid the groundwork for racial antisemitism

5. Racial antisemitism that arose in the 19th century and culminated in Nazism in the 20th century

6. Contemporary antisemitism, which has been labeled by some as the "[New Antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Antisemitism)"

Chanes suggests that these six stages could be merged into three categories: "ancient antisemitism, which was primarily ethnic in nature; [Christian antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_antisemitism), which was religious; and the racial antisemitism of the nineteenth and twentieth centuries."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChanes20045–6-163]

### Ancient world

The first clear examples of anti-Jewish sentiment can be traced to the 3rd century BCE to [Alexandria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandria),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlannery198511-164] the home to the largest Jewish diaspora community in the world at the time and where the [Septuagint](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Septuagint), a Greek translation of the [Hebrew Bible](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_Bible), was produced. [Manetho](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manetho), an Egyptian priest and historian of that era, wrote scathingly of the Jews. His themes are repeated in the works of [Chaeremon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaeremon_of_Alexandria), [Lysimachus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lysimachus), [Poseidonius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poseidonius), [Apollonius Molon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apollonius_Molon), and in [Apion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apion) and [Tacitus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tacitus).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlannery200412-165] [Agatharchides of Cnidus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agatharchides_of_Cnidus) ridiculed the practices of the Jews and the "absurdity of [their Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torah)", making a mocking reference to how [Ptolemy Lagus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ptolemy_Lagus) was able to invade [Jerusalem](16043.md) in 320 BCE because its inhabitants were observing the *[Shabbat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shabbat)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlannery200412-165] One of the earliest anti-Jewish [edicts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edict), promulgated by [Antiochus IV Epiphanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antiochus_IV_Epiphanes) in about 170–167 BCE, sparked a revolt of the [Maccabees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maccabees) in [Judea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judea).[^cite_note-gruen-166]

In view of Manetho's anti-Jewish writings, antisemitism may have originated in Egypt and been spread by "the [Greek](42056.md) retelling of [Ancient Egyptian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Egypt) prejudices".[^cite_note-Schäfer-167] The ancient Jewish philosopher [Philo of Alexandria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Philo_of_Alexandria) describes an attack on Jews in Alexandria in 38 CE in which thousands of Jews died.[^cite_note-Barclay-168][^cite_note-169] The violence in Alexandria may have been caused by the Jews being portrayed as [misanthropes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misanthropy).[^cite_note-vanderhorst-170] Tcherikover argues that the reason for hatred of Jews in the Hellenistic period was their separateness in the Greek cities, the *[poleis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polis)*.[^cite_note-tcherikover-171] Bohak has argued, however, that early animosity against the Jews cannot be regarded as being anti-Judaic or antisemitic unless it arose from attitudes that were held against the Jews alone, and that many Greeks showed animosity toward any group they regarded as barbarians.[^cite_note-Bohak-172]

Statements exhibiting prejudice against Jews and their religion can be found in the works of many [pagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pagan) [Greek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Greece) and [Roman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Rome) writers.[^cite_note-173] Edward Flannery writes that it was the Jews' refusal to accept Greek religious and social standards that marked them out. Hecataetus of Abdera, a Greek historian of the early third century BCE, wrote that Moses "in remembrance of the exile of his people, instituted for them a misanthropic and inhospitable way of life." Manetho wrote that the Jews were expelled Egyptian [lepers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leprosy) who had been taught by [Moses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moses) "not to adore the gods."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlannery198511–12-174] Edward Flannery describes antisemitism in ancient times as essentially "cultural, taking the shape of a national xenophobia played out in political settings."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlannery198524–26-175]

There are examples of [Hellenistic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellenistic) rulers desecrating the [Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Temple_in_Jerusalem) and banning Jewish religious practices, including [Antiochus IV Epiphanes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antiochus_IV_Epiphanes), who in 167 BCE erected an altar to Zeus in the temple directed the sacrifice of pigs to at the altar.[^cite_note-EB-176][^cite_note-cdlib-177] He also banned Jewish practices such as circumcision, Shabbat observance, and the study of Torah scrolls, sparking the Maccabean Revolt.[^cite_note-cdlib-177]

The Jewish diaspora on the [Nile](21244.md) island [Elephantine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elephantine), which was founded by mercenaries, experienced the destruction of its temple in 410 BCE.[^cite_note-178]

[Relationships between the Jewish people and the occupying Roman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_the_Roman_Empire) were at times antagonistic and resulted in [several rebellions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish-Roman_wars), most notably the [First Jewish–Roman War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Jewish%E2%80%93Roman_War) and the [Bar Kokhba revolt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bar_Kokhba_revolt).[^cite_note-179] According to [Suetonius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suetonius), the emperor [Tiberius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tiberius) took measures against Jews in Rome in 19 CE, expelling and relocating some Jewish‑affiliated groups in response to unrest and alleged religious‑moral offenses.[^cite_note-180] The 18th-century English historian [Edward Gibbon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Gibbon) identified a more tolerant period in Roman–Jewish relations beginning in about 160 CE. However, with the rise of Christianity as the dominant religion, [increasingly restrictive legislation and rhetoric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_antisemitism#Late_Roman_Empire) was directed against Jews.[^cite_note-181]

[James Carroll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Carroll_\(novelist\)) asserted: "Jews accounted for 10% of the total population of the Roman Empire. By that ratio, if other factors such as [pogroms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pogrom) and [conversions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forced_conversion) had not intervened, there would be 200 million Jews in the world today, instead of something like 13 million."[^cite_note-182]

### Persecutions during the Middle Ages

Main articles: [History of European Jews in the Middle Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_European_Jews_in_the_Middle_Ages) and [Antisemitism in Islam § Pre-modern Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_Islam#Pre-modern_Islam)

In the late 6th century CE, the newly Catholicised Visigothic kingdom in Hispania issued a series of anti-Jewish edicts which forbade Jews from marrying Christians, practicing circumcision, and observing Jewish holy days.[^cite_note-183] Continuing throughout the 7th century, both Visigothic kings and the Church were active in creating social aggression and towards Jews with "civic and ecclesiastic punishments",[^cite_note-184] ranging between forced conversion, slavery, exile and death.[^cite_note-185]

From the 9th century, the [medieval Islamic world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Golden_Age) classified Jews and Christians as *[dhimmis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dhimmis)* and allowed Jews to practice their religion more freely than they could do in [medieval Christian Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages). Under [Islamic rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Andalus), there was a [Golden age of Jewish culture in Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_age_of_Jewish_culture_in_Spain) that lasted until at least the 11th century.[^cite_note-186] It ended when several Muslim [pogroms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pogrom) against Jews took place on the [Iberian Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iberian_Peninsula), including those that occurred in [Córdoba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C3%B3rdoba,_Spain) in 1011 and in [Granada in 1066](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1066_Granada_massacre).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerrySchweitzer2002267–268-187][^cite_note-188][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarzigHoerderShubert200342-189] Several decrees ordering the destruction of synagogues were also enacted in Egypt, Syria, Iraq and Yemen from the 11th century. In addition, Jews were forced to convert to Islam or face death in some parts of Yemen, Morocco and Baghdad several times between the 12th and 18th centuries.[^cite_note-190]

The [Almohads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Almohad_Caliphate), who had taken control of the [Almoravids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Almoravid_dynasty)' [Maghribi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maghreb) and Andalusian territories by 1147,[^cite_note-islamicworldeb-191] were far more fundamentalist in outlook compared to their predecessors, and they treated the *dhimmis* harshly. Faced with the choice of either death or conversion, many Jews and Christians emigrated.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrankLeaman2003137–138-192][^cite_note-193][^cite_note-194] Some, such as the family of [Maimonides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maimonides), fled east to more tolerant Muslim lands,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrankLeaman2003137–138-192] while some others went northward to settle in the growing Christian kingdoms.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrankLeaman2003137–138-192]



[Expulsions of Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expulsions_and_exoduses_of_Jews) in Europe from 1100 to 1600

In [medieval](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_Ages) Europe, Jews were persecuted with [blood libels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_libel), expulsions, [forced conversions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forced_conversion) and [massacres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massacre). These persecutions were often justified on religious grounds and reached a first peak during the [Crusades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crusades). In 1096, hundreds or thousands of [Jews were killed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhineland_massacres) during the [First Crusade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Crusade).[^cite_note-195] This was the first major outbreak of anti-Jewish violence in Christian Europe outside Spain and was cited by Zionists in the 19th century as indicating the need for a state of Israel.[^cite_note-196]

In 1147, there were several massacres of Jews during the [Second Crusade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Crusade). The [Shepherds' Crusades of 1251](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shepherds'_Crusade_\(1251\)) and [1320](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shepherds'_Crusade_\(1320\)) both involved attacks, as did the [Rintfleisch massacres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rintfleisch_massacres) in 1298. Expulsions followed, such as the 1290 banishment of Jews from England, the expulsion of 100,000 Jews from France in 1394,[^cite_note-197] and the 1421 expulsion of thousands of Jews from Austria. Many of the expelled Jews fled to Poland.[^cite_note-198]

In medieval and Renaissance Europe, a major contributor to the deepening of antisemitic sentiment and legal action among the Christian populations was the popular preaching of the zealous reform religious orders, the Franciscans (especially [Bernardino of Feltre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernardino_of_Feltre)) and Dominicans (especially [Vincent Ferrer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincent_Ferrer)), who combed Europe and promoted antisemitism through their often fiery, emotional appeals.[^cite_note-199]

As the [Black Death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Death) epidemics devastated Europe in the mid-14th century, causing the death of a large part of the population, Jews were used as [scapegoats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scapegoating#Psychology_and_sociology). Rumors spread that they caused the disease by deliberately poisoning wells. Hundreds of Jewish communities were [destroyed in numerous persecutions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Death_Jewish_persecutions). Although [Pope Clement VI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_Clement_VI) tried to protect them by issuing two [papal bulls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Papal_bull) in 1348, the first on 6 July and an additional one several months later, 900 Jews were [burned alive in Strasbourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strasbourg_massacre), where the plague had not yet affected the city.[^cite_note-Black-200]

### Reformation

Main article: [Martin Luther and antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Luther_and_antisemitism)

[Martin Luther](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Luther), an [ecclesiastical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecclesiastical) reformer whose teachings inspired the [Reformation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant_Reformation), wrote antagonistically about Jews in his pamphlet *[On the Jews and their Lies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/On_the_Jews_and_Their_Lies_\(Martin_Luther\))*, written in 1543. He portrays the Jews in extremely harsh terms, excoriates them and provides detailed recommendations for a [pogrom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pogrom) against them, calling for their permanent oppression and expulsion. At one point he writes: "...we are at fault in not slaying them...", a passage that, according to historian [Paul Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paul_Johnson_\(writer\)), "may be termed the first work of modern antisemitism, and a giant step forward on the road to [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust)."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJohnson1987242-201]

### 17th century



Etching of the [expulsion of the Jews from Frankfurt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frankfurter_Judengasse#The_Fettmilch_Uprising) in 1614

During the mid-to-late 17th century the [Polish–Lithuanian Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish%E2%80%93Lithuanian_Commonwealth) was devastated by several conflicts, in which the Commonwealth lost over a third of its population (over 3 million people), and Jewish losses were counted in the hundreds of thousands. The first of these conflicts was the [Khmelnytsky Uprising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khmelnytsky_Uprising), when [Bohdan Khmelnytsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bohdan_Khmelnytsky)'s supporters massacred tens of thousands of [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Jews_in_Poland) in the eastern and southern areas he controlled (today's [Ukraine](31750.md)). The precise number of dead may never be known, but the decrease of the Jewish population during that period is estimated at 100,000 to 200,000, which also includes emigration, deaths from diseases, and [captivity in the Ottoman Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slavery_\(Ottoman_Empire\)), called *jasyr*.[^cite_note-202][^cite_note-203]

European immigrants to the United States brought antisemitism to the country as early as the 17th century. [Peter Stuyvesant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peter_Stuyvesant), the Dutch governor of [New Amsterdam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Amsterdam), implemented plans to prevent Jews from settling in the city. During the Colonial Era, the American government limited the political and economic rights of Jews. It was not until the [American Revolutionary War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War) that Jews gained legal rights, including the right to vote. However, even at their peak, the restrictions on Jews in the United States were never as stringent as they had been in Europe.[^cite_note-204]

In the [Zaydi imamate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zaydi) of [Yemen](350939.md), Jews were also singled out for discrimination in the 17th century, which culminated in the general expulsion of all Jews from places in Yemen to the arid coastal plain of [Tihamah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tihamah) and which became known as the [Mawza Exile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mawza_Exile).[^cite_note-205]

### Enlightenment

In 1744, Archduchess of Austria [Maria Theresa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Theresa_of_Austria) ordered Jews out of [Bohemia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bohemia) but soon reversed her position, on the condition that Jews pay for their readmission every ten years. This [extortion](162449.md) was known among the Jews as *[malke-geld](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malke-geld)* ("queen's money" in Yiddish).[^cite_note-206] In 1752, she introduced the law limiting each Jewish family to one son.

In 1782, [Joseph II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_II,_Holy_Roman_Emperor) abolished most of these persecution practices in his *[Toleranzpatent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toleranzpatent)*,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien196929-207][^cite_note-208] on the condition that [Yiddish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yiddish_language) and [Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language) were eliminated from public records and that judicial autonomy was annulled.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEO'Brien196930-209] [Moses Mendelssohn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moses_Mendelssohn) wrote that "Such a tolerance... is even more dangerous play in tolerance than open persecution."

#### Voltaire

According to [Arnold Ages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnold_Ages), [Voltaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voltaire)'s "Lettres philosophiques, Dictionnaire philosophique, and Candide, to name but a few of his better known works, are saturated with comments on Jews and Judaism and the vast majority are negative".[^cite_note-210] Paul H. Meyer adds: "There is no question but that Voltaire, particularly in his latter years, nursed a violent hatred of the Jews and it is equally certain that his animosity...did have a considerable impact on public opinion in France."[^cite_note-211][^cite_note-212] Thirty of the 118 articles in Voltaire's *[Dictionnaire Philosophique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dictionnaire_Philosophique)* concerned Jews and described them in consistently negative ways.[^cite_note-213]

### Louis de Bonald and the Catholic Counter-Revolution

The [counter-revolutionary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Counter-revolutionary) Catholic royalist [Louis de Bonald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_de_Bonald) stands out among the earliest figures to explicitly call for the reversal of Jewish emancipation in the wake of the [French Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Revolution).[^cite_note-Battini1-214][^cite_note-215] Bonald's attacks on the Jews are likely to have influenced [Napoleon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleon)'s decision to limit the civil rights of Alsatian Jews.[^cite_note-216][^cite_note-217] Bonald's article *Sur les juifs* (1806) was one of the most venomous screeds of its era and furnished a paradigm which combined anti-liberalism, a defense of a rural society, traditional Christian antisemitism, and the identification of Jews with bankers and finance capital, which would in turn influence many subsequent right-wing reactionaries such as [Roger Gougenot des Mousseaux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Gougenot_des_Mousseaux), [Charles Maurras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Maurras), and [Édouard Drumont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89douard_Drumont), nationalists such as [Maurice Barrès](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurice_Barr%C3%A8s) and [Paolo Orano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paolo_Orano), and antisemitic socialists such as [Alphonse Toussenel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alphonse_Toussenel).[^cite_note-Battini1-214][^cite_note-218][^cite_note-219] Bonald furthermore declared that the Jews were an "alien" people, a "state within a state", and should be forced to wear a distinctive mark to more easily identify and discriminate against them.[^cite_note-Battini1-214][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMichael2008128–129-220]

Under the French Second Empire, the popular counter-revolutionary Catholic journalist [Louis Veuillot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Veuillot) propagated Bonald's arguments against the Jewish "financial aristocracy" along with vicious attacks against the Talmud and the Jews as a "deicidal people" driven by hatred to "enslave" Christians.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMichael2008128–129-220][^cite_note-221] Between 1882 and 1886 alone, French priests published twenty antisemitic books blaming France's ills on the Jews and urging the government to consign them back to the ghettos, expel them, or hang them from the gallows.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMichael2008128–129-220] Gougenot des Mousseaux's *Le Juif, le judaïsme et la judaïsation des peuples chrétiens* (1869) has been called a "Bible of modern antisemitism" and was translated into German by Nazi ideologue [Alfred Rosenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Rosenberg).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMichael2008128–129-220]

### Russian Empire

Main article: [Antisemitism in the Russian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_the_Russian_Empire)



The victims of the 1903 [Kishinev pogrom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kishinev_pogrom), Russian Empire (modern-day Moldova)

Thousands of Jews were slaughtered by Cossack [Haidamaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haidamaka) in the 1768 [massacre of Uman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massacre_of_Uman), in the [Kingdom of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crown_of_the_Kingdom_of_Poland). In 1772, the empress of Russia, [Catherine II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catherine_the_Great), forced the Jews into the [Pale of Settlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pale_of_Settlement)—which was located primarily in present-day Poland, Ukraine, and Belarus—and to stay in their [shtetls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shtetls) and forbade them from returning to the towns that they occupied before the [partition of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Partition_of_Poland). From 1804, Jews were banned from their villages and began to stream into the towns.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJohnson1987358-222] A decree by emperor [Nicholas I of Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicholas_I_of_Russia) in 1827 conscripted Jews under 18 years of age into the [cantonist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantonist) schools for a 25-year military service in order to promote baptism.[^cite_note-223]

Policy towards Jews was liberalised somewhat under [Czar Alexander II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czar_Alexander_II) (r. 1855–1881).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEJohnson1987359-224] However, his assassination in 1881 served as a pretext for further repression, such as the [May Laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_Laws) of 1882. [Konstantin Pobedonostsev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Konstantin_Pobedonostsev), a tutor to the [czarevitch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czarevitch), later crowned [Czar Nicholas II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czar_Nicholas_II), declared that "One-third of the Jews must die, one-third must emigrate, and one third be converted to Christianity".[^cite_note-225]

*[The Protocols of the Elders of Zion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Protocols_of_the_Elders_of_Zion)*, published in Russia in 1903, was an antisemitic forgery. Widely circulated and translated, it became a powerful propaganda weapon for antisemitic elements worldwide. [Henry Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Ford) sponsored its circulation in the United States. It claimed a secret Jewish cabal was taking over the world.[^cite_note-226]

Since late 19th century numerous [pogroms in the Russian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pogroms_in_the_Russian_Empire) occurred, contributing to the [massive emiration of the Jews from Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Russia#Mass_emigration).

A number of [blood libel cases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_libel#19th_century) were reported in the Russian Empire in the 19th cetury.

### Islamic antisemitism in the 19th century

Historian [Martin Gilbert](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_Gilbert) writes that it was in the 19th century that the position of Jews worsened in [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim) countries. [Benny Morris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benny_Morris) writes that one symbol of Jewish degradation was the phenomenon of stone-throwing at Jews by Muslim children. Morris quotes a 19th-century traveler: "I have seen a little fellow of six years old, with a troop of fat toddlers of only three and four, teaching \[them] to throw stones at a Jew, and one little urchin would, with the greatest coolness, waddle up to the man and literally spit upon his Jewish [gaberdine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaberdine). To all this the Jew is obliged to submit; it would be more than his life was worth to offer to strike a Mahommedan."[^cite_note-Morris10-227]

In the middle of the 19th century, [J. J. Benjamin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._J._Benjamin) wrote about the life of [Persian Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_Jews), describing conditions and beliefs that went back to the 16th century: "…they are obliged to live in a separate part of town… Under the pretext of their being unclean, they are treated with the greatest severity and should they enter a street, inhabited by Mussulmans, they are pelted by the boys and mobs with stones and dirt…."[^cite_note-228]

In Jerusalem at least, conditions for some Jews improved. [Moses Montefiore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moses_Montefiore), on his seventh visit in 1875, noted that fine new buildings had sprung up and, "surely we're approaching the time to witness God's hallowed promise unto Zion." Muslim and Christian Arabs participated in [Purim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Purim) and [Passover](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passover); Arabs called the [Sephardis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sephardi) 'Jews, sons of Arabs'; the [Ulema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ulema) and the Rabbis offered joint prayers for rain in time of drought.[^cite_note-229]

At the time of the Dreyfus trial in France, "Muslim comments usually favoured the persecuted Jew against his Christian persecutors".[^cite_note-230]

### Secular or racial antisemitism



Title page of the second edition of *Das Judenthum in der Musik*, published in 1869



Antisemitic agitators in Paris burn an effigy of Mathieu Dreyfus during the [Dreyfus affair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dreyfus_affair)

In 1850, the German composer [Richard Wagner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Wagner)—who has been called "the inventor of modern antisemitism"[^cite_note-bismarck-231]—published *[Das Judenthum in der Musik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Das_Judenthum_in_der_Musik)* (roughly "Jewishness in Music")[^cite_note-bismarck-231] under a [pseudonym](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pseudonym) in the *[Neue Zeitschrift für Musik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neue_Zeitschrift_f%C3%BCr_Musik)*. The essay began as an attack on Jewish composers, particularly Wagner's contemporaries, and rivals, [Felix Mendelssohn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felix_Mendelssohn) and [Giacomo Meyerbeer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giacomo_Meyerbeer), but expanded to accuse Jews of being a harmful and alien element in [German culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Germany), who corrupted morals and were, in fact, parasites incapable of creating truly "German" art. The crux was the manipulation and control by the Jews of the money economy:[^cite_note-bismarck-231]

> According to the present constitution of this world, the Jew in truth is already more than emancipated: he rules, and will rule, so long as Money remains the power before which all our doings and our dealings lose their force.[^cite_note-bismarck-231]

Although originally published anonymously, when the essay was republished 19 years later, in 1869, the concept of the corrupting Jew had become so widely held that Wagner's name was affixed to it.[^cite_note-bismarck-231]

Antisemitism can also be found in many of the [Grimms' Fairy Tales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grimms'_Fairy_Tales) by [Jacob](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacob_Grimm) and [Wilhelm Grimm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelm_Grimm), published from 1812 to 1857.[^cite_note-White-2025-232][^cite_note-Helfer-2021-233] It is mainly characterized by Jews being the [villain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Villain) of a story, such as in "The Good Bargain" ("*Der gute Handel*") and "[The Jew Among Thorns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jew_Among_Thorns)" ("*Der Jude im Dorn*").[^cite_note-White-2025-232][^cite_note-Helfer-2021-233]

The middle 19th century saw continued official harassment of the Jews, especially in Eastern Europe under Czarist influence. For example, in 1846, 80 Jews approached the governor in Warsaw to retain the right to wear their traditional dress but were immediately rebuffed by having their hair and beards forcefully cut, at their own expense.[^cite_note-BDE-234]

Even such influential figures as [Walt Whitman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walt_Whitman) tolerated bigotry toward the Jews in America. During his time as editor of the Brooklyn Eagle (1846–1848), the newspaper published historical sketches casting Jews in a bad light.[^cite_note-BDE-Whitman-235]

The [Dreyfus Affair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dreyfus_Affair) was an infamous antisemitic event of the late 19th century and early 20th century. [Alfred Dreyfus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Dreyfus), a Jewish artillery [captain](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/captain) in the [French Army](611740.md), was accused in 1894 of passing secrets to the Germans. As a result of these charges, Dreyfus was convicted and sentenced to [life imprisonment](148681.md) on [Devil's Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devil's_Island). The actual spy, Marie Charles Esterhazy, was acquitted. The event caused great uproar among the French, with the public choosing sides on the issue of whether Dreyfus was actually guilty or not. [Émile Zola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89mile_Zola) accused the army of corrupting the French justice system. However, general consensus held that Dreyfus was guilty: 80% of the press in France condemned him. This attitude among the majority of the French population reveals the underlying antisemitism of the time period.[^cite_note-236]

[Adolf Stoecker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolf_Stoecker) (1835–1909), the [Lutheran](23371382.md) court chaplain to [Kaiser Wilhelm I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaiser_Wilhelm_I), founded in 1878 an antisemitic, [anti-liberal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberalism) political party called the [Christian Social Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Social_Party_\(Germany\)).[^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238] This party always remained small, and its support dwindled after Stoecker's death, with most of its members eventually joining larger conservative groups such as the [German National People's Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_National_People's_Party).

Some scholars view [Karl Marx](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karl_Marx)'s essay "[On The Jewish Question](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/On_The_Jewish_Question)" as antisemitic, and argue that he often used antisemitic epithets in his published and private writings.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFlannery2004168-239][^cite_note-Jacobs2005-240][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELewis1999112-241] These scholars argue that Marx equated Judaism with capitalism in his essay, helping to spread that idea. Some further argue that the essay influenced [National Socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazism), as well as Soviet and Arab antisemites.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPerrySchweitzer2005154–157-242][^cite_note-Stav2003-243][^cite_note-Muravchik2003-244] Marx himself had Jewish ancestry, and [Albert Lindemann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Lindemann) and [Hyam Maccoby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyam_Maccoby) have suggested that he was [embarrassed by it](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-hating_Jew).[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246]

Others argue that Marx consistently supported Prussian Jewish communities' struggles to achieve equal political rights. These scholars argue that "On the Jewish Question" is a critique of Bruno Bauer's arguments that Jews must convert to Christianity before being emancipated, and is more generally a critique of liberal rights discourses and capitalism.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcLellan1980141–142-247][^cite_note-248] Iain Hampsher-Monk wrote that "This work \[On The Jewish Question] has been cited as evidence for Marx's supposed anti-Semitism, but only the most superficial reading of it could sustain such an interpretation."[^cite_note-249]

David McLellan and [Francis Wheen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francis_Wheen) argue that readers should interpret *On the Jewish Question* in the deeper context of Marx's debates with [Bruno Bauer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Bauer), author of *[The Jewish Question](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Jewish_Question)*, about [Jewish emancipation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_emancipation) in Germany. Wheen says that "Those critics, who see this as a foretaste of 'Mein Kampf', overlook one, essential point: in spite of the clumsy phraseology and crude stereotyping, the essay was actually written as a defense of the Jews. It was a retort to Bruno Bauer, who had argued that Jews should not be granted full civic rights and freedoms unless they were baptised as Christians".[^cite_note-250] According to McLellan, Marx used the word *Judentum* colloquially, as meaning *commerce*, arguing that Germans must be emancipated from the [capitalist mode of production](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitalist_mode_of_production_\(Marxist_theory\)) not Judaism or Jews in particular. McLellan concludes that readers should interpret the essay's second half as "an extended pun at Bauer's expense".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMcLellan1980142-251]

### 20th century

See also: [Jewish Bolshevism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Bolshevism), [Racial policy of Nazi Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_policy_of_Nazi_Germany), and [Soviet anti-Semitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_anti-Semitism)



Public reading of the antisemitic newspaper *[Der Stürmer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Der_St%C3%BCrmer)*, [Worms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Worms,_Germany), Germany, 1935

Between 1900 and 1924, approximately 1.75 million Jews migrated to America, the bulk from Eastern Europe escaping [the pogroms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pogroms_in_the_Russian_Empire).[^cite_note-252][^cite_note-253] This increase, combined with the [upward social mobility](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upward_social_mobility) of some Jews, contributed to a resurgence of antisemitism. In the first half of the 20th century, in the US, Jews were discriminated against in employment, access to residential and resort areas, membership in clubs and organizations, and in tightened quotas on Jewish enrolment and teaching positions in colleges and universities. The lynching of [Leo Frank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leo_Frank) by a mob of prominent citizens in [Marietta, Georgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marietta,_Georgia), in 1915 turned the spotlight on antisemitism in the United States.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChanes2004\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidju7U83nRDt8CpgPA72_72\]-254] The case was also used to build support for the renewal of the [Ku Klux Klan](16779.md) which had been inactive since 1870.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELevy2005\[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidTdn6FFZklkcCpgPA243_vol._1,_p. 72\]-255]

At the beginning of the 20th century, the [Beilis Trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menahem_Mendel_Beilis) in Russia represented modern incidents of [blood-libels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_libel) in Europe. During the [Russian Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Civil_War), close to 50,000 Jews were [killed in pogroms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pogroms_of_the_Russian_Civil_War).[^cite_note-256]

Antisemitism in America reached its peak during the [interwar period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interwar_period). The pioneer automobile manufacturer [Henry Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Ford) propagated antisemitic ideas in his newspaper *[The Dearborn Independent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Dearborn_Independent)* (published by Ford from 1919 to 1927). The radio speeches of [Father Coughlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Father_Coughlin) in the late 1930s attacked [Franklin D. Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt)'s [New Deal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Deal) and promoted the notion of a Jewish financial conspiracy. Some prominent politicians shared such views: [Louis T. McFadden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_T._McFadden), Chairman of the [United States House Committee on Banking and Currency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_House_Committee_on_Banking_and_Currency), blamed Jews for Roosevelt's decision to abandon the [gold standard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gold_standard), and claimed that "in the United States today, the Gentiles have the slips of paper while the Jews have the lawful money".[^cite_note-257]



A wagon piled high with corpses outside the crematorium at the recently liberated [Buchenwald concentration camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buchenwald_concentration_camp), 1945

In Germany, shortly after [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md) and the [Nazi Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_Party) [came to power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machtergreifung) in 1933, the government instituted repressive legislation which denied Jews basic civil rights.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMajer201460-258][^cite_note-259]

In September 1935, the Nuremberg Laws prohibited sexual relations and marriages between "Aryans" and Jews as Rassenschande ("race defilement") and stripped all German Jews, including many quarter- and half-Jews (Mischlinge), of full citizenship, reclassifying them as "protected subjects of the state" (Staatsangehörige).[^cite_note-260][^cite_note-261] These laws culminated in a state-sponsored pogrom on the night of 9–10 November 1938, known as Kristallnacht ("Night of Broken Glass"), during which at least 91 Jews were killed, thousands arrested, over 7,500 Jewish businesses destroyed, and about 1,400 synagogues burned or damaged.[^cite_note-262][^cite_note-\:0-263] Antisemitic legislation, agitation, and propaganda were subsequently extended to German-occupied Europe following the onset of war in 1939, frequently adapting or amplifying local antisemitic traditions (e.g., in Vichy France and occupied Poland).[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-\:0-263]

In 1940, the famous aviator [Charles Lindbergh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Lindbergh) and many prominent Americans led the [America First Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/America_First_Committee) in opposing any involvement in a European war. Lindbergh alleged that Jews were pushing America to go to war against Germany.[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267] Lindbergh adamantly denied being antisemitic, and yet he refers numerous times in his private writings—his letters and diary—to Jewish control of the media being used to pressure the U.S. to get involved in the European war. In one diary entry in November 1938, he responded to *Kristallnacht* by writing "I do not understand these riots on the part of the Germans. ... They have undoubtedly had a difficult Jewish problem, but why is it necessary to handle it so unreasonably?", acknowledgement on Lindbergh's part that he agreed with the Nazis that Germany had a "Jewish problem".[^cite_note-268] An article by Jonathan Marwil in *Antisemitism, A Historical Encyclopedia of Prejudice and Persecution* claims that "no one who ever knew Lindbergh thought him antisemitic" and that claims of his antisemitism were solely tied to the remarks he made in that one speech.[^cite_note-269]

In the east the Third Reich forced Jews into ghettos [in Warsaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Ghetto), [in Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krak%C3%B3w_Ghetto), [in Lvov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lw%C3%B3w_Ghetto), [in Lublin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lublin_Ghetto) and [in Radom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radom_Ghetto).[^cite_note-270] After [the beginning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Operation_Barbarossa) of the war between Nazi Germany and the Soviet Union in 1941, a campaign of mass murder, conducted by the [Einsatzgruppen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Einsatzgruppen), culminated from 1942 to 1945 in systematic [genocide](12441.md): [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust).[^cite_note-saul1-271] Eleven million Jews were targeted for extermination by the Nazis, and some six million were eventually killed.[^cite_note-saul1-271][^cite_note-272][^cite_note-273]

### Soviet antisemitism

See also: [Soviet anti-Zionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_anti-Zionism) and [Soviet anti-Semitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_anti-Semitism)

There have continued to be antisemitic incidents since WWII, some of which had been state-sponsored. In the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union), antisemitism was even used as an instrument for settling personal conflicts, starting with the conflict between [Joseph Stalin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Stalin) and [Leon Trotsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leon_Trotsky) and continuing through numerous conspiracy theories spread by official propaganda. [Antisemitism in the USSR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_the_Soviet_Union) reached new heights after 1948 during the campaign against the "[rootless cosmopolitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rootless_cosmopolitan)" (euphemism for "Jew") in which numerous Yiddish-language poets, writers, painters, and sculptors were killed or arrested.[^cite_note-jcws-274][^cite_note-Myth-275] This culminated in the antisemitic conspiracy theory of the '[Doctors' Plot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctors'_Plot)' in 1952.[^cite_note-276][^cite_note-277]

In the 20th century, [Soviet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_the_Soviet_Union) and [Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_Russia) antisemitism underwent significant transformations, shaped by political, social, and ideological shifts. During the early Soviet period, the [Bolsheviks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bolsheviks) initially condemned antisemitism, seeing it as incompatible with [Marxist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marxism) ideology. However, under [Joseph Stalin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Stalin)'s regime, antisemitism reemerged, often cloaked in 'anti-Zionist' rhetoric. As early as 1943, Stalin and his propagandists intensified attacks against Jews as "[rootless cosmopolitans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rootless_cosmopolitan)".[^cite_note-SO-278] The Party issued confidential directives to fire Jews from positions of power, but state-controlled media did not openly attack Jews until the late 1940s.[^cite_note-SO-278] The [Doctors' plot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctors'_plot) of 1952, a fabricated conspiracy accusing predominantly Jewish doctors of attempting to assassinate Soviet leaders, exemplified this resurgence. This campaign fostered widespread antisemitic sentiments and resulted in the arrest and execution of numerous Jewish professionals.

In that same year, the antisemitic [Slánský show trial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sl%C3%A1nsk%C3%BD_trial) alleged the existence of an 'international Zionist conspiracy' to destroy Socialism. Izabella Tabarovsky, a scholar of the history of antisemitism, argues that, "Manufactured by the Soviet secret services, the trial tied together Zionism, Israel, Jewish leaders, and American imperialism, turning 'Zionism' and 'Zionist' into dangerous labels that could be used against one's political enemies."[^cite_note-AZ-279] In the post-Stalin era, state-sanctioned antisemitism persisted and intensified. In February 1953, the Soviet Union severed diplomatic relations with the [State of Israel](9282173.md) and "soon the state media was saturated with anti-Zionist propaganda, depicting bloated, hook-nosed Jewish bankers and all-consuming serpents embossed with the Star of David."[^cite_note-280] The 1963 publication of the antisemitic book *[Judaism Without Embellishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism_Without_Embellishment),* written under orders from the central Soviet government, echoed [Nazi propaganda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Propaganda_in_Nazi_Germany), alleging a global Jewish conspiracy to subvert the Soviet Union.[^cite_note-AZ-279]

The [Six-Day War](29328.md) in 1967 led to an intensification in Soviet anti-Zionist propaganda as the Soviets had backed the defeated Arab states.[^cite_note-AZ-279] This propaganda often blurred the lines with antisemitism, leading to discriminatory policies against Jews and restricting their emigration. By the end of the war, "the "corporate Jew", whether "cosmopolitan" or "Zionist", became identified as the enemy. Popular anti-Semitic stereotyping had been absorbed into official channels, generated by chauvinist needs and totalitarian requirements."[^cite_note-AA-281] The [Anti-Zionist Committee of the Soviet Public](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Zionist_Committee_of_the_Soviet_Public) shut down and expropriated [synagogues](48559.md), [yeshivas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeshiva), and Jewish civil organisations and prohibited the learning of [Hebrew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hebrew_language).[^cite_note-AZ-279] It also engaged in a wide-scale propaganda campaign between 1967 and 1988 overseen by the [KGB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KGB) and published pamphlets featuring antisemitic conspiracy theories, for example falsely claiming that Zionist Jews collaborated with the Nazi regime in the Holocaust and of inflating the significance and scale of anti-Jewish persecution.[^cite_note-AZ-279]

Their propaganda frequently borrowed directly from the forged [Protocols of the Elders of Zion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Protocols_of_the_Elders_of_Zion) and sometimes relied upon [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md)'s *[Mein Kampf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mein_Kampf)* as a source of information about Zionism.[^cite_note-AZ-279] Antizionism helped Moscow "bond both with its Arab allies and the Western hard left of all shades. Having appointed Zionism as a scapegoat for humanity's greatest evils, Soviet propaganda could score points by equating it with racism in African radio broadcasts and with Ukrainian nationalism on Kyiv TV."[^cite_note-DB-282] The still-extant [Novosti Press Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novosti_Press_Agency), a key element in the Soviet propaganda machine, also participated in the spreading of antisemitic anti-Zionism. Its chairman, Ivan Udaltsov, published a memorandum on 27 January 1971, to the [CPSU](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_the_Soviet_Union) in which he claimed that "Zionists, by provoking antisemitism, recruit volunteers for the Israeli army", blaming Jews for antisemitism, and falsely alleged that Zionists were responsible for "subversive activities" during the 1968 [Prague Spring](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prague_Spring).[^cite_note-DB-282] According to historian [William Korey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Korey), "Judaism was singled out for condemnation as prescribing 'racial exclusivism' and as justifying 'crimes against 'Gentiles.'"[^cite_note-AA-281]

Similar [antisemitic propaganda in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_Poland) resulted in the flight of Polish Jewish survivors from the country.[^cite_note-Myth-275] After the war, the [Kielce pogrom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kielce_pogrom) and the "[March 1968 events](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/March_1968_events)" in communist Poland represented further incidents of antisemitism in Europe. The [anti-Jewish violence in postwar Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Jewish_violence_in_Poland,_1944%E2%80%931946) had a common theme of [blood libel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_libel) rumours.[^cite_note-283][^cite_note-284]

## Contemporary antisemitism

### The contemporary far-right

Antisemitism is still prevalent in contemporary far-right politics across much of the globe, whether as overt claims or covert dog-whistles.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESubotic2022459-285][^cite_note-286] Antisemitism has been a key factor for multiple perpetrators of far-right violence and terrorism in recent years, including the [Pittsburgh synagogue shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pittsburgh_synagogue_shooting), the [2018 United States mail bombing attempts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_United_States_mail_bombing_attempts), and the [Poway synagogue shooting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poway_synagogue_shooting).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEByington201910-287][^cite_note-288] While many far-right parties that express antisemitism have seen increasing electoral success, especially in Europe,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFeinbergStewart2019192–193-289][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESchut2025-290][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHenley2024-291] a noted development in the rhetoric and positions of these parties from their far-right predecessors has been their increasing support of Zionism, while maintaining antisemitic views of diaspora Jews.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESubotic2022460-292] [Judith Butler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judith_Butler), [Masha Gessen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masha_Gessen), and others have identified a mainstreaming of [Zionist antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionist_antisemitism) from the far-right under the Trump administrations in the US,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEButler2017182–183-293][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGessen2019-294] with the [Zionist Organization of America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionist_Organization_of_America) honouring [Steve Bannon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steve_Bannon) in 2017.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStephens2017-295][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBaroudRubeo2018-296]

### Holocaust denial

Main article: [Holocaust denial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial)

Holocaust denial, the claim that the Nazi genocide of European Jews during the [Second World War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_World_War) either never happened or is substantially exaggerated by historical accounts, is a form of antisemitism and conspiracy theory.[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-ADL_Denial-157] Political movements seeking to revive the ideologies of the Nazis and other states that participated in the Holocaust, like [neo-Nazism](54361.md) and [neofascism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neofascism), practice Holocaust denial.[^cite_note-Atkins-298][^cite_note-299] Rehabilitation of Nazism can also be accomplished on an individual scale. For example, in October 2015, Israeli Prime Minister [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md) caused commotion for saying the Palestinian Grand Mufti of Jerusalem, [Haj Amin al-Husseini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haj_Amin_al-Husseini) gave [Adolf Hitler](2731583.md), the [dictator](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dictator) of [Germany during the Nazi era](21212.md), the idea of exterminating Jews rather than expelling them during the [Second World War](32927.md).[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301] Academic [Moshe Zimmermann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Zimmermann) stated that "any attempt to deflect the burden from Hitler to others is a form of Holocaust denial."[^cite_note-302]

There is significant debate about whether [analogies between Israel's treatment of Palestinians and the Nazis' treatment of Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comparisons_between_Israel_and_Nazi_Germany) or comparisons to [Anti-Palestinianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Palestinianism) are antisemitic.[^cite_note-303][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERosenfeld2019175–178,_186-304] Those who say they are antisemitic have termed such analogies "Holocaust inversion"—a form of [Holocaust trivialisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_trivialisation), in which the Holocaust is compared with other events in a way that downplays its severity.[^cite_note-marcus-305] [Deborah Lipstadt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deborah_Lipstadt) describes Holocaust inversion as a type of Holocaust denial.[^cite_note-306]

### 21st-century European antisemitism

Further information: [Antisemitism in Europe § 21st century](16938566.md#21st_century)

Physical assaults against Jews in Europe have included beatings, stabbings, and other violence, which increased markedly, sometimes resulting in serious injury and death.[^cite_note-mgjmsp-307][^cite_note-308] A 2015 report by the US State Department on religious freedom declared that "European anti-Israel sentiment crossed the line into anti-Semitism."[^cite_note-309]

This rise in antisemitic attacks is associated with both [Muslim antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_Islam) and the rise of far-right political parties as a result of the economic crisis of 2008.[^cite_note-310] This rise in the support for far-right ideas in [western](33800.md) and [eastern Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Europe) has resulted in the increase of antisemitic acts, mostly attacks on Jewish memorials, synagogues and cemeteries but also a number of physical attacks against Jews.[^cite_note-311]

In Eastern Europe the dissolution of the Soviet Union and the instability of the new states brought the rise of nationalist movements and the accusation against Jews for the economic crisis, taking over the local economy and bribing the government, along with traditional and religious motives for antisemitism such as [blood libels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_libel).[^cite_note-312][^cite_note-313] Writing on the [rhetoric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhetoric) surrounding the [2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](79400268.md), [Jason Stanley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jason_Stanley) relates these perceptions to broader historical narratives: "the dominant version of antisemitism alive in parts of eastern Europe today is that Jews employ the Holocaust to seize the victimhood narrative from the 'real' victims of the Nazis, who are Russian Christians (or other non-Jewish eastern Europeans)".[^cite_note-Stanley_2022-314] He calls out the "myths of contemporary eastern European antisemitism—that a global cabal of Jews were (and are) the real agents of violence against Russian Christians and the real victims of the Nazis were not the Jews, but rather this group."[^cite_note-Stanley_2022-314]

Most of the antisemitic incidents in Eastern Europe are against Jewish cemeteries and buildings (community centers and synagogues). Nevertheless, there were several violent attacks against Jews in Moscow in 2006 when a neo-Nazi stabbed 9 people at the Bolshaya Bronnaya Synagogue,[^cite_note-315] the failed bomb attack on the same synagogue in 1999,[^cite_note-316] the threats against Jewish pilgrims in Uman, Ukraine[^cite_note-317] and the attack against a [menorah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menorah_\(Temple\)) by extremist Christian organization in Moldova in 2009.[^cite_note-318]

According to Paul Johnson, antisemitic policies are a sign of a state which is poorly governed.[^cite_note-319] While no European state currently has such policies, the [Economist Intelligence Unit](15303116.md) notes the rise in political uncertainty, notably populism and nationalism, as something that is particularly alarming for Jews.[^cite_note-Cohen-320]

### 21st-century Arab antisemitism

Main article: [Antisemitism in the Arab world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_the_Arab_world)



[Graffiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graffiti) of a [swastika](45943.md) on a building in the [Palestinian](23267.md) city of [Nablus](233369.md), 2022

[Robert Bernstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_L._Bernstein), founder of [Human Rights Watch](46530.md), says that antisemitism is "deeply ingrained and institutionalized" in "Arab nations in modern times".[^cite_note-321]

In a 2011 survey by the [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center), all of the Muslim-majority Middle Eastern countries polled held significantly negative opinions of Jews. In the questionnaire, only 2% of [Egyptians](8087628.md), 3% of [Lebanese](17771.md) Muslims, and 2% of [Jordanians](7515964.md) reported having a positive view of Jews. Muslim-majority countries outside the Middle East similarly held markedly negative views of Jews, with 4% of [Turks](11125639.md) and 9% of [Indonesians](14579.md) viewing Jews favorably.[^cite_note-322]

According to a 2011 exhibition at the United States Holocaust Memorial Museum in Washington, United States, some of the dialogue from Middle East media and commentators about Jews bear a striking resemblance to [Nazi propaganda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_propaganda).[^cite_note-323] According to Josef Joffe of *[Newsweek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newsweek)*, "anti-Semitism—the real stuff, not just bad-mouthing particular Israeli policies—is as much part of Arab life today as the hijab or the hookah. Whereas this darkest of creeds is no longer tolerated in polite society in the West, in the Arab world, Jew hatred remains culturally endemic."[^cite_note-324]

Muslim clerics in the Middle East have frequently referred to Jews as descendants of apes and pigs, which are conventional epithets for Jews and Christians.[^cite_note-325][^cite_note-326][^cite_note-327]

According to professor [Robert Wistrich](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Wistrich), director of the [Vidal Sassoon International Center for the Study of Antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vidal_Sassoon_International_Center_for_the_Study_of_Antisemitism) (SICSA), the calls for the destruction of Israel by [Iran](14653.md) or by [Hamas](13913.md), [Hezbollah](13919.md), [Islamic Jihad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Jihad_Movement_in_Palestine), or the [Muslim Brotherhood](20742.md), represent a contemporary mode of genocidal antisemitism.[^cite_note-328]

### 21st-century antisemitism at universities

Main article: [Universities and antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universities_and_antisemitism)

.After the [2023 Hamas-led attack on Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Hamas-led_attack_on_Israel) on 7 October and Israel's subsequent invasion of the Gaza strip, antisemitism and anti-Jewish hate crimes around the world increased significantly.[^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330][^cite_note-331] Multiple universities and university officials have been accused of systemic antisemitism.[^cite_note-332][^cite_note-333][^cite_note-334] On 1 May 2024, the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md) voted 320–91 in favour of adopting a bill enshrining the [International Holocaust Remembrance Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Holocaust_Remembrance_Alliance) definition of antisemitism into law.[^cite_note-335] A statement opposing the proposal was signed by 1,200 Jewish professors, who said it conflated criticism of Israel with antisemitism,[^cite_note-336] while Jewish advocacy groups like the [American Jewish Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Jewish_Committee) and [World Jewish Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Jewish_Congress) generally supported it in response to the increase in antisemitic incidents on university campuses.[^cite_note-337][^cite_note-338]

### Black Hebrew Israelite antisemitism

Further information: [Black Hebrew Israelites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Hebrew_Israelites)

Relevant discussion may be found on the [talk page](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk\:Antisemitism#). [You can help](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Antisemitism\&action=edit). The talk page may contain suggestions. *(August 2026)* |
| --- | --- |



[Black Hebrew Israelites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Hebrew_Israelites) in 2019.

[^cite_note-JB-339]

Black Hebrew Israelites believe that Jewish people are "imposters", who have "stolen" Black Americans' true racial and religious identity.[^cite_note-HI-340][^cite_note-George_Washington_2022-341] Some of these groups also promote the unsupported [Khazar hypothesis of Ashkenazi ancestry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khazar_hypothesis_of_Ashkenazi_ancestry).[^cite_note-HI-340] In 2022, the American Jewish Committee stated that the Black Hebrew Israelite claim that "we are the real Jews" is a "troubling antisemitic trope with dangerous potential," arguing that such rhetoric perpetuates antisemitism by denying Jewish people's legitimacy as Jews.[^cite_note-342][^cite_note-343][^cite_note-JB-339]

In September 2022, the Program on Extremism at George Washington University published a report which said the largest threat came from "individuals loosely affiliated with or inspired by the movement", rather than from formal members of Black Hebrew Israelite organizations.[^cite_note-JB-339][^cite_note-George_Washington_2022-341]

## Causes

|  | This section **may contain [original research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:No_original_research)**. Antisemitism has been explained in terms of [racism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racism), [xenophobia](34141.md), [projected guilt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychological_projection), [displaced aggression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Displaced_aggression), [conspiracy theory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conspiracy_theory), and the search for a [scapegoat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scapegoating).[^cite_note-344]

Antisemitism scholar Lars Fischer writes that "scholars distinguish between theories that assume an actual causal (rather than merely coincidental) correlation between what (some) Jews do and antisemitic perceptions (correspondence theories), on the one hand, and those predicated on the notion that no such causal correlation exists and that 'the Jews' serve as a foil for the projection of antisemitic assumptions, on the other."[^cite_note-u458-345] The latter position is exemplified by [Theodor W. Adorno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodor_W._Adorno), who wrote that "Anti-Semitism is the rumour about the Jews"; in other words, "a conspiratorial mentality that sees Jewish people as invisible and yet ubiquitous, as capable of pulling the strings of power from behind the scenes."[^cite_note-346][^cite_note-347]

As an example of the correspondence theory, an 1894 book by [Bernard Lazare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bernard_Lazare) questions whether Jews themselves were to blame for some antisemitic stereotypes, for instance arguing that Jews traditionally keeping strictly to their own communities, with their own practices and laws, led to a perception of Jews as anti-social; he later abandoned this belief and the book is considered antisemitic today.[^cite_note-348][^cite_note-349][^cite_note-q406-350] As another example, [Walter Laqueur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walter_Laqueur) suggested that the antisemitic perception of Jewish people as greedy (as often used in [stereotypes of Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stereotypes_of_Jews#Greed)) probably evolved in Europe during medieval times where a large portion of [money lending](601598.md) was operated by Jews.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELaqueur2006154-351] Among factors thought to contribute to this situation include that Jews were restricted from other professions,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELaqueur2006154-351] while the [Christian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Church) declared for their followers that money lending constituted immoral "[usury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Usury)",[^cite_note-352] although recent scholarship, such as that of historian Julie Mell shows that Jews were not overrepresented in the sector and that the stereotype was founded in Christian [projection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Psychological_projection) of taboo behaviour on to the minority.[^cite_note-u458-345][^cite_note-s525-353][^cite_note-a817-354]

In *Anti-Judaism: The Western Tradition* (2013), historian [David Nirenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Nirenberg) traces the history of antisemitism, arguing that antisemitism should be understood not as a product of isolated historical events or cultural biases but is instead embedded within the very fabric of Western thought and society.[^cite_note-AJ-355] Its foundation lies in the early claim of [Jewish deicide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_deicide) and depictions of Jews as 'Christ-killers'. Throughout Western history, Jews have since been used as a symbolic '[other](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Other_\(philosophy\))' to define and articulate the values and boundaries of various cultures and intellectual traditions. In philosophy, literature, and politics, Jewishness has often been constructed as a counterpoint to what is considered normative or ideal. One of the key insights from Nirenberg's work is that antisemitism has proven to be remarkably adaptable. It changes form and adapts to different contexts and times, whether in medieval religious disputes, Enlightenment critiques, or modern racial theories. Philosophers and intellectuals have often used 'Jewishness' as a foil to explore and define their ideas. For instance, in the [Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Age_of_Enlightenment), figures like [Voltaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voltaire) critiqued Judaism as backward and superstitious to promote their visions of reason and progress. Similarly, the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union) frequently [portrayed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_anti-Semitism) Judaism as linked with capitalism and mercantilism, standing in opposition to the ideals of proletarian solidarity and [communism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communism). In each case, Judaism or the Jews are portrayed as standing in tension with prevailing moral norms.[^cite_note-AJ-355]

&#x20;Author and scholar [Dara Horn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dara_Horn) published an article in *[The Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Atlantic)* reflecting on the rising antisemitism in the wake of the Hamas-led [October 7 attacks](74998317.md) in Israel and her previous published doubts about the effectiveness of [Holocaust education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_education) [pedagogy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedagogy), specifically citing a personal interaction with a Holocaust educator in which an "anti-Semitic myth comparable to Holocaust denial" was communicated to her, due to what she felt was insufficient contextualization of the Holocaust in the [history of antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_antisemitism).[^cite_note-PS-356] In it, Horn argues that antisemitism functions by appropriating what has happened to Jews and recasting their experience as part of a broader, universal struggle, which always ends in ultimately redefining Jewish identity as incompatible with these ideals. She concludes that the attacks on Jews, often under the guise of anti-Zionism, follow the same ancient pattern of marginalization and vilification.

> This is the permission structure for anti-Semitism: claim whatever has happened to the Jews as one's own experience, announce a "universal" ideal that all good people must accept, and then redefine Jewish collective identity as lying beyond it. Hating Jews thus becomes a demonstration of righteousness. The key is to define, and redefine, and redefine again, the shiny new moral reasoning for why the Jews have failed the universal test of humanity.[^cite_note-PS-356]

## Prevention through education

Education plays an important role in addressing and overcoming [prejudice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prejudice) and countering social [discrimination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discrimination).[^cite_note-GP-357] However, education is not only about challenging the conditions of intolerance and ignorance in which antisemitism manifests itself; it is also about building a sense of [global citizenship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_citizenship_education) and solidarity, respect for, and enjoyment of diversity and the ability to live peacefully together as active, democratic citizens. Education equips learners with the knowledge to identify antisemitism and biased or prejudiced messages and raises awareness about the forms, manifestations, and impact of antisemitism faced by [Jews](25955086.md) and Jewish communities.[^cite_note-GP-357]

Some Jewish writers have argued that public education about antisemitism through the prism of the [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust) is unhelpful at best or actively deepening antisemitism at worst. [Dara Horn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dara_Horn) wrote in *[The Atlantic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Atlantic)* that "Auschwitz is not a metaphor", arguing "That the Holocaust drives home the importance of love is an idea, like the idea that Holocaust education prevents anti-Semitism, that seems entirely unobjectionable. It is entirely objectionable. The Holocaust didn't happen because of a lack of love. It happened because entire societies abdicated responsibility for their own problems, and instead blamed them on the people who represented—have always represented, since they first introduced the idea of commandedness to the world—the thing they were most afraid of: responsibility."[^cite_note-358]

Instead, she argues that perhaps "a more effective way to address anti-Semitism might lie in cultivating a completely different quality, one that happens to be the key to education itself: curiosity. Why use Jews as a means to teach people that we're all the same, when the demand that Jews be just like their neighbors is exactly what embedded the mental virus of anti-Semitism in the Western mind in the first place? Why not instead encourage inquiry about the diversity, to borrow a de rigueur word, of the human experience?"[^cite_note-359]

## Geographical variation

Main article: [Geography of antisemitism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_antisemitism)

A March 2008 report by the [U.S. State Department](31975.md) found that there was an increase in antisemitism across the world, and that both old and new expressions of antisemitism persist.[^cite_note-360] A 2012 report by the U.S. [Bureau of Democracy, Human Rights and Labor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Democracy,_Human_Rights_and_Labor) also noted a continued global increase in antisemitism, and found that Holocaust denial and opposition to Israeli policy at times was used to promote or justify blatant antisemitism.[^cite_note-361] In 2014, the Anti-Defamation League conducted a study titled *ADL Global 100: An Index of Anti-Semitism*,[^cite_note-362] which also reported high antisemitism figures around the world and, among other findings, that as many as "27% of people who have never met a Jew nevertheless harbor strong prejudices against him".[^cite_note-TOI-ADL-363]

In August 2024, the Israeli [Ministry of Diaspora Affairs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Diaspora_Affairs) announced a new antisemitism monitoring project.[^cite_note-Diaspora1-364][^cite_note-Diaspora2-365] The goal of the project is to measure levels of antisemitism in various countries, as well as identify instigators and trends.[^cite_note-Diaspora1-364] In the event that antisemitism in a given country becomes severe, the Diaspora Minister may contact that country's government in an official capacity.[^cite_note-Diaspora1-364]

A July 2026 report by the J7 Large Communities’ Task Force Against Antisemitism found that 2025, with more than 20 people killed in antisemitic incidents in the seven countries studied, was the deadliest year for antisemitic violence in more than 30 years. The task force tracks antisemitic violence in Argentina, Australia, Canada, France, Germany, the United Kingdom and the United States, which are home to the largest Jewish communities outside of Israel. “Antisemitism in our seven countries is no longer a surge; it is our new normal,” the report said.[^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367]

[^cite_note-2]: &#x20;Also spelled **anti-semitism** or **anti-Semitism**; The [International Holocaust Remembrance Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Holocaust_Remembrance_Alliance) has stated that the spelling without hyphenation is preferred, because the spelling with hyphenation implies that "[Semitism](https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/Semitism)" is a valid concept.<sup>[\[1\]](#cite_note-IHRA2-1)</sup>

[^cite_note-7]: &#x20;See the [§ Eternalism–contextualism debate](#Eternalism–contextualism_debate) paragraph.

[^cite_note-16]: &#x20;Notable instances of antisemitic persecution include the [Rhineland massacres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhineland_massacres) in 1096; the [Edict of Expulsion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edict_of_Expulsion) in 1290; the [European persecution of Jews during the Black Death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persecution_of_Jews_during_the_Black_Death), between 1348 and 1351; the [massacre of Spanish Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Massacre_of_1391) in 1391, the crackdown of the [Spanish Inquisition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Inquisition), and the [expulsion of Jews from Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alhambra_Decree) in 1492; the [Cossack massacres in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tach_VeTat), between 1648 and 1657; various [anti-Jewish pogroms in the Russian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pogroms_in_the_Russian_Empire), between 1821 and 1906; the [Dreyfus affair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dreyfus_affair), between 1894 and 1906; [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust) by [Nazi Germany](21212.md) during [World War II](32927.md); and various [Soviet anti-Jewish policies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_the_Soviet_Union).

[^cite_note-IHRA2-1]: &#x20;["Memo on Spelling of Antisemitism"](https://www.holocaustremembrance.com/sites/default/files/memo-on-spelling-of-antisemitism_final-1.pdf) (PDF). Retrieved 24 May 2019. The unhyphenated spelling is favored by many scholars and institutions in order to dispel the idea that there is an entity 'Semitism' which 'anti-Semitism' opposes.

[Executive Council of Australian Jewry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Council_of_Australian_Jewry). p. 9. Archived from [the original](http://www.ecaj.org.au/wp-content/uploads/2012/08/2014_antisemitism_report.pdf) (PDF) on 12 April 2015. Retrieved 27 October 2018.

[^cite_note-10]: &#x20;["Antisemitism in History: Racial Antisemitism, 1875–1945"](https://encyclopedia.ushmm.org/content/en/article/antisemitism-in-history-racial-antisemitism-18751945). *[United States Holocaust Memorial Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Holocaust_Memorial_Museum)*. [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20200331191034/https://www.ushmm.org/wlc/en/article.php?ModuleId=10007171) from the original on 31 March 2020. Retrieved 20 September 2023. These new 'antisemites,' as they called themselves, drew upon older stereotypes to maintain that the Jews behaved the way they did—and would not change—because of innate racial qualities inherited from the dawn of time. Drawing as well upon the pseudoscience of racial [eugenics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugenics), they argued that the Jews spread their so-called pernicious influence to weaken nations in [Central Europe](5188.md) not only by political, economic, and media methods, but also literally by 'polluting' so-called pure [Aryan blood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aryan_race) by intermarriage and sexual relations with non-Jews. They argued that Jewish 'racial intermixing,' by 'contaminating' and weakening the host nations, served as part of a conscious [Jewish plan for world domination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Jewish_conspiracy).

[112](https://books.google.com/books?id=tYW013SjKM4C\&pg=PA112): "The term "anti-Semitism" was unsuitable from the beginning for the real essence of Jew-hatred, which remained anchored, more or less, in the Christian tradition even when it moved, via the natural sciences, into racism. It is doubtful whether the term which was first publicized in an institutional context (the Anti-Semitic League) would have appeared at all if the "Anti-Chancellor League," which fought Bismarck's policy, had not been in existence since 1875. The founders of the new Organization adopted the elements of "anti" and "league," and searched for the proper term: Marr exchanged the term "Jew" for "Semite" which he already favored. It is possible that the shortened form "Sem" is used with such frequency and ease by Marr (and in his writings) due to its literary advantage and because it reminded Marr of Sem Biedermann, his Jewish employer from the Vienna period."

[^cite_note-Sevenster1975-90]: &#x20;Sevenster, Jan Nicolaas (1975). [*The Roots of Pagan Anti-Semitism in the Ancient World*](https://books.google.com/books?id=yLE3AAAAIAAJ\&pg=PA1). Brill Archive. pp. 1–2. [ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-90-04-04193-6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-90-04-04193-6). It has long been realised that there are objections to the term anti-Semitism and therefore an endeavour has been made to find a word which better interprets the meaning intended. Already in 1936 Bolkestein, for example, wrote an article on Het "antisemietisme" in de oudheid (Anti-Semitism in the ancient world) in which the word was placed between quotation marks and a preference was expressed for the term hatred of the Jews… Nowadays the term anti-Judaism is often preferred. It certainly expresses better than anti-Semitism the fact that it concerns the attitude to the Jews and avoids any suggestion of racial distinction, which was not or hardly, a factor of any significance in ancient times. For this reason Leipoldt preferred to speak of anti-Judaism when writing his Antisemitsmus in der alten Welt (1933). Bonsirven also preferred this word to Anti-Semitism, "mot moderne qui implique une théorie des races".

Lazare argued in his book that Jews, because of their exclusiveness, arrogance, and unsociability, were themselves responsible for anti-Semitism. Lazare blames the Jewish religion and laws for these negative traits. His book was widely reviewed and is by many accounts a seminal anti-Semitic text. Lazare's authorship of such an anti-Semitic work is ironic, given the role he would soon play in the Dreyfus Affair.
